Chapter 4 Cyber Physical System in Medical Science

NITIN TYAGI, POONAM RANI, SIDDHARTH GAUTAM, and PRIYANSHU JOSHI

Department of Computer Science and Engineering, GGSIPU, Delhi, India

Abstract

Medical cyber physical systems (MCPS) are critical-to-life systems with the priority of ensuring patient safety. They guarantee the validation of technologies and propose a safer and more efficient approach to health-care, aiming to increase productivity without compromising treatment effectiveness. The basic approach is to utilize existing technologies in an organized and advanced manner, expanding their reach. MCPS is a part of the cyber physical system (CPS) trend, which is already solving numerous problems worldwide. MCPS includes patient monitoring through various devices and introduces newer technologies. It prioritizes safety and transparency in work, addressing previously overlooked or easily managed issues. This chapter presents a comprehensive overview of MCPS and CPS and explores how their implementation will benefit the modern world.

4.1 Introduction

The cyber physical systems (CPS) indicate the new generation of systems that have unified capabilities and functionalities of interacting with humans and bringing closeness to the virtual world with the real world. Over the years, research has been carried out to find ways to ease the tasks that humans find exhausting and time-consuming [1]. CPS not only reduce the efforts of humans but also provide extra functionality with the devices and greater efficiency. Industries and companies have developed various systems that best suit the situation in which the work extraction is doubled while the workload remains the same [2]. The increasing complexity of advanced systems and technologies challenges us to get the best out of them and prepare along with the best efforts possible. The need to build next-gen control systems has now become a necessity. The CPS need a structured model of tools and mechanisms to fulfil the needs of the manufacturer and the consumer. Computer Science has made enough advancements in the field of modern technology and in the upcoming times, we are expecting it to diversify [3]. The CPS will play a major role in various domains that will help mankind achieve heights that were not possible a few years back [4]. We need the right theories and tools to implement the CPS accurately and help us get cost-effective, reliable, and efficient resources so as to resolve the problems easily and at a quicker speed [5]. The basic steps to be taken care of while developing a CPS include designing, analyzing, and verifying the used components, system, and software architecture, conducting regular stage checks to monitor performance, efficiency, accuracy, and areas of improvement. CPS research is still in its early years, with isolated attention given to sensor devices, communication and networking devices, control theories, mathematical calculations, software engineering, and computer science. By focusing on these areas, we can extract the best machinery ever produced, which will help improve current standards of living, healthcare, and modern requirements that affect our daily lives. The advancement in the field of CPS can be accelerated by identifying opportunities, challenges, and needs in various sectors of industrial development [6]. The objective is to build new technologies that are compatible, energy-efficient, low maintenance, and most importantly, efficient. The security of the user must also be a concern, and the device or machinery must comply with the rules of development.

Cyber physical systems (CPS) in the medical field refer to a critical network of healthcare services. Instead of standalone devices currently used in healthcare, which monitor or suggest treatment for individuals, CPS will soon be enhanced to monitor multiple patients and assist healthcare workers simultaneously. This will involve a coherent display of patients’ health conditions, joining the virtual world with the real world. Data, medicine, prescriptions, and basic details will be easily and securely accessible only to authorized personnel [7]. This revolutionary trend has greatly impacted the CPS sector.

The development of a safe and secure medical cyber physical system (MCPS) requires perfect design, architecture, structured planning, and validation and verification techniques. Due to the increased complexity and size of this sector, model-based technology is necessary. MCPS designs also encompass communication and devices within the entire scenario. MCPS will open up new opportunities in the healthcare sector, not only in terms of advancement but also cost-effectiveness. However, obtaining approval for the use of MCPS in patient treatment requires a new regulatory and managed procedure. The current process-based regulatory regime used by the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) is quite extensive, costly, and complex. Figure 4.1 illustrates the workflow of a medical organization and depicts the actual process of analyzing the credibility of an individual.

The remaining chapter is organized as follows: Section 4.2 discusses the taxonomy of MCPS. In Section 4.3, the devices currently in use will be discussed. Section 4.4 will include the challenges faced by the MCPS systems and how they can be solved, as well as the extent to which we can extract the best out of them. Section 4.5 includes the advantages of MCPS that will help improve our healthcare sector, and Section 4.6 will contain the conclusion along with the future scope of MCPS.

4.2 Categorization Of Cps In Health Sector

The devices/systems displayed above are discussed in detail below. These systems contribute to the improvement of MCPS as a whole and suggest the ongoing technologies in the market, along with the changes that can be made to enhance them. Figure 4.2 depicts the taxonomy of CPS in health-care, which includes six systems or devices.

4.2.1 Electronic Medical Records (EMR)

Electronic medical records (EMR) refer to the structure of a virtual-physical interface used for automatic readings of vital signs. This interface serves as a solution to the time-consuming and error-prone process of monitoring. The design of the interface allows the network to collect and store data into the EMR system. The key elements of this design include the EMR, a software adapter known as the data handler, and a vital sign reading station based on hardware topology [8]. The system follows a three-tier architecture, as discussed in the SDD, to address the virtual-physical approach. This approach ensures that changes made to components in one layer do not affect the components in other layers [9]. The implementation of EMR offers several benefits. Patient data can be tracked over a specific timeline, and it can be accessed by authorized healthcare contributors. This facilitates the identification of patients who are due for routine check-ups and appointments, as well as monitoring their specific needs such as vaccinations and blood pressure readings. EMRs are designed to support organizations in providing systematic and accurate care to patients.

4.2.2 CPeSC3

Proposed by Wang et al. the layout of CPeSC3 consists of three major components: Communication Core, Computation Core, Resource Scheduling, and Management Core. Cloud computing technology is adopted, specifically in reference to healthcare applications. A healthcare app scenario is presented here, which is established on a practical test bed for confirmation purposes [10]. The advantages of CPeSC3 include increased functionality and validation of treatments provided by physicians. It enables the healthcare system to easily monitor large amounts of data, reduce redundancy, and provide efficient service in a timely manner.

4.2.3 CYPSec

Proposed by Venkatasubramanian et al. cyber physical security (CYPSec) is a solution that is synchronized with other security solutions, incorporating traditional security measures and environmental aspects [11]. The design principles and challenges of CYPSec can be illustrated through two examples: Physiological signal-based key agreement (PSKA) and critically aware access control (CAAC). PSKA facilitates automatic key agreement between sensor devices in the body area network (BAN) based on physiological signals from the body. On the other hand, CAAC provides authority to establish and manage emergency situations responsibly. CYPSec offers several advantages. It combines traditional methodologies with modern methods, ensuring the security of patient data and fostering trust in organizations. The data is not easily manipulated, guaranteeing accurate and effective healthcare services.

4.2.4 Virtual-Physical Wban System through Social Network

Zhang et al. propose an approach that utilizes power games to mitigate communication disruptions in WBANs. This approach is based on the information that encompasses social interactions among individuals. The contribution is made in the following areas: structuring inter-WBAN disturbances and calculating the distance distribution of interruptions, creating algorithms for detecting and predicting social communications for individuals carrying WBANs [12]. The Cyber-Physical WBAN System using Social Networks has several advantages: it enhances interaction, reduces the distance between patients and doctors, allows healthcare service providers to closely engage with patients’ families, and provides better guidance for the future. Technological advancements have streamlined the interaction between patients and doctors, making it easier [13].

4.2.5 MCPS and Big Data Platform

A Big Data processing infrastructure for MCPS is proposed by Don & Dugki, which integrates the physical world with dynamics and provides flexibility to the virtual world in healthcare decision-making systems. This framework claims to reduce hospital expenses and simplify routine checkups conducted by clinic doctors. Additionally, it will help enhance facilities in remote areas by transmitting data collected from ECG machines, oxygen levels, and pulse rates through various sensors to a remote application server. Physicians can then analyze this data to monitor the condition of all patients and prescribe accordingly [14]. Benefits: Big Data in MCPS establishes a fully aware system, ensuring efficient and troublefree delivery. This system requires various subsystems to perform tasks such as filtering, listening, processing, accelerating, and enrichment, ulti-mately creating a fully equipped system and enhancing overall functionality [15]. Big Data is a growing trend, and with the right resources and guidance, it can flourish for the betterment of the healthcare sector and improve delivery.

4.2.6 Smart Checklist

A Smart Checklist is proposed by Avrunin et al. to support and assist participants with their respective tasks. This system also facilitates the interaction between devices and software applications. When used in intensive care, this system is intended to aid medical professionals in medication administration, data collection, and other standard activities for patients. However, the system is not yet applicable to all medical procedures and requires further modification [16]. The advantages of Smart Checklist include improved assistance, faster responses, and more efficient results. Smart Checklist helps enhance services and reduces the likelihood of errors, which is another benefit of using it.

4.3 Devices Used In Mcps

The devices used in healthcare not only ease the doctor’s and staff’s work but also provide convenience to the patients. The devices available in the market enable the patients to track their health, previous records, appointments, and other medical-related stuff online. This has revolutionized the whole system and has given a better approach for the same [17]. The devices used in MCPS are listed in subsections. Figure 4.3 shows the devices in a systematic behavior.

4.3.1 Mobihealth

The system is designed to collect data from smart wearables that monitor your medical status. In the event of an emergency, it also attempts to collect audio and video signals. The data is analyzed by smart sensors and sent to system servers. If there are any irregular readings, the servers are alerted. This can enhance response time and reaction during accidents or other emergency situations.

4.3.2 Codeblue

It is a tenet merged with the following biomedical sensors: (i) pulse oximeter oxygen saturation measurement device; (ii) two-lead ECG; and (iii) motion sensor with pub-sub-based routing framework-based software architecture. Its job is to efficiently manage and communicate among medical devices [19]. This system promotes the early use of smart routing and in-network aggregation.

4.3.3 Alarm-Net

It is a wireless system of bio-sensory devices that illustrates networks consisting of the following: (i) pulse rate; (ii) heart rate; (iii) oximetry evaluation; and (iv) ECG. Spatial contextual data is provided through environmental parameters such as temperature and humidity. The system also takes into consideration privacy, power management, and queries. It utilizes a pathway between the information collection and the storage unit [20]. Additionally, this system includes a GUI to assist healthcare professionals in determining the patient’s vital signs.

4.3.4 Mobile Ecg

This ECG architecture utilizes smartphones as an interface and a data collector, while a home device serves as a measurement tool for ECG and analysis. The smartphones receive ECG data from mobile ECG recording devices via Bluetooth. If deemed necessary, the smartphones analyze and transmit the data to medical professionals [21]. The graphical interface also has the capability to display ECG readings and pulse rate.

4.3.5 Vital Signs Prediction

This ECG architecture utilizes smartphones as an interface and a data collector, while a home device serves as a measurement tool for ECG and analysis. The smartphones receive ECG data from mobile ECG recording devices via Bluetooth. If deemed necessary, the smartphones analyze and transmit the data to medical professionals [21, 22]. The graphical interface also has the capability to display ECG readings and pulse rate.

4.3.6 Medicines Consumption Application

It can take care of people when they face analytical disabilities.

4.3.6.1 Icabinet

Smart RFID packaging is utilized in the case of iCabiNET, which detects the extraction of a tablet when an electrical flow is broken down in the RFID circuit. The system of medicine intake is observed using the RFID reader through a residential network available at homes [23]. If you use smart appliances with this system, you can also monitor the necessity to purchase medications that are running low in your supplies. Additionally, it can remind you to take your medications on time and with accurate dosage [24].

4.3.6.2 Ipackage

Different from RFID packaging, this is a prototype that features intelligent packaging, proposed by Pang et al. This system monitors medication intake along with vital signs. The system utilizes an array of controlled delamination material (CDM) films that incorporate unified control circuits. The CDM film consists of three layers: aluminum lower and upper layers, and a sticking mid-layer of electrochemical epoxy [25]. An electrochemical reaction occurs within the mid-layer when a voltage greater than a specific energy is applied to the lower (bottom) layer [26]. All of these technicalities ensure that the iPackage can only be accessed through an essential governing device, which also authorizes power over the dosage to prevent accidents or misuse.

4.4 Challenges in MCPS

We will discuss the challenges in CPS and then move on to the challenges faced in MCPS. Challenges in CPS range from the lack of CPS standards to the architecture of CPS [27]. Figure 4.4 provides an overview of the challenges faced in MCPS as well as CPS. We can gain a better understanding of the challenges in MCPS only when we have an idea of the challenges faced in CPS.

4.4.1 Challenges Faced in Cyber Physical Systems (CPS)

4.4.1.1 Lacking CPS Standard

Identifying the information-based explanations and calculations for theories such as security, privacy, and safety is a scientifically challenging task [28]. It brings uncertainty in how the utilization and further reasoning will be carried out, even if the concepts are identified. For example, even professionals are entrusted with patient-doctor secrecy and the data privacy of patients [29]. A question arises as to what level of concern privacy will be considered breached. This clearly highlights how the CPS standard lacks in modern-day situations where privacy is of utmost importance.

4.4.1.2 Lacking Confirmation and Tools of Validation

We can utilize a CPS application to integrate the Wireless Sensor Network (WSN) and cloud computing architecture. Despite the availability of WSN and Cloud Computing modeling and simulation tools, there is a need for new simulation tools to comprehensively design the CPS system. These tools should be capable of handling the gathering, analysis, processing, and activation of heterogeneous sensors, communication layers, and other data types within the system [30]. This ensures the system remains integrated and responsive.

4.4.1.3 Management of Time in Architectectural Design

In CPS, time management is a crucial issue due to the application of cloud integration and heterogeneous sensors. Given the need for real-time applications in MCPS, time management takes top priority. This includes time scaling, time synchronization, determining reference time, and interacting with various sensor devices while considering the characteristics of time [31]. An error in time management can have disastrous consequences, as lives may be at stake. Therefore, time management must be flawless in every single case.

4.4.1.4 Architecture of CPS

The challenges faced in CPS arise from the complexity of computing and physical dynamics. These challenges include time management, data correctness, and standards. The need of the hour is a common or consolidated architecture that can address all the aforementioned issues. Such an architecture will play a crucial role in the future of CPS design.

4.4.2 Challenges Faced in MCPS

4.4.2.1 Reliability of Software

The program (software) is an important asset of healthcare devices, which ensures the functionality of devices and other functions. It also manages the proper coordination between patients and medical devices. Therefore, the safety and efficient management rely on proper software design and development. The system also improves when the software is more responsive, maintains a good history of records, and remains accurate at all times. With everything falling into place, a perfect healthcare system can be achieved.

4.4.2.2 Interoperability of Medical Devices

Various medical devices are supposed to have distinct communication interfaces for smooth communication. We require a management system to oversee this and ensure that all the diverse medical devices are protected, reliable, and verified. Interoperability enables devices to function better and prevents accidents and emergency situations caused by haphazard communication. This managed approach will ensure effectiveness and avoid any adverse effects on the users, whether they are health-care providers or patients.

4.4.2.3 Extraction of Data

The collection of data from multiple patients by medical devices through various physiological criteria helps provide a wide variety of basic information about patients. It also helps in preventing future sickness and is effective in nullifying emergency situations. Although designing a device that seamlessly extracts physiological data from patients is challenging, it would be a revolutionary device. Many such devices are already in development.

4.4.2.4 Security and Privacy

Privacy is the foremost thing to be considered when it comes to organizing and handling patient data. The data of patients is blindly trusted with the healthcare system, so the system is expected to be trustworthy. Forfeiture of private data and details can even lead to mental unrest and abuse, which can further damage a patient’s health. Privacy can be improved by implementing end-to-end encryption in the system. Encryption ensures that there is no third party who might tamper with or misuse the data. This can ensure proper functioning and smoothness in the system. Many systems currently in use rely on offline files and paperwork, which can be messy. Adopting organized ways can enhance security and patient satisfaction.

4.4.2.5 System Feedback

Developing a feedback system becomes challenging for MCPS due to the lack of design stability without the feedback system or improvement of patient care. MCPS has implemented a robust feedback system using a quick-witted alarm system. This alarm device is of utmost importance in alerting the care provider for potential threats, illnesses, or any emergency scenarios. However, the alarm system faces several challenges such as determining the types of physiological parameters, dealing with structural complexity, and ensuring effective implementation to enhance overall functionality. It is necessary to carefully select the relevant physiological variables for planning the alarm system, considering the wide range of illnesses. This task can be exhausting due to the need to detect specific sicknesses from various probabilities [32].

4.4.2.6 Complex Query Processing

Wireless sensors operated on batteries pose challenges due to the availability of heterogeneous biosensors, including issues such as lower power consumption and limited refining capabilities that hinder compound query processing. Complex Query Processing is very helpful in reducing the amount of transmission and enabling context-aware predictions, which in turn lowers power consumption in a limited energy arrangement. We can use this approach to access various physiological variables and precalculate possible sickness. However, handling this approach requires a complex design and algorithmic skill set.

4.4.2.7 Lack of Prototype Architecture in MCPS

In the current scenario, the architecture lacks safe and dependable prototypes for experimentation, evaluation, and system enhancements that include devices in the health sector. This is yet another factor that contributes to the inability to ensure the correctness of the MCPS architecture in this uncertain environmental scenario.

4.5 Advantages of MCPS

There are numerous advantages of technologies in the modern world, and a few of the advantages of MCPS [33] are listed in subsections. Figure 4.5 illustrates how technologies have affected and brought about a boom in the healthcare sector. Now, let’s discuss the advantages of MCPS, which include network integration and various other benefits.

4.5.1 Integration of Network

CPS is said to have integration with WSNs and cloud computing, which can offer consistency in associating levels. Various algorithmic platforms communicating over communication networks are also included in CPS. Integration of networks also improves the overall system communication.

4.5.2 Improved Interaction Between Human and System

It is necessary to comprehend the needs and design the structure accordingly. The adaptability to surrounding changes in variables that are critical for decision making is also enhanced. Thus, it also becomes essential for complex and dynamic systems. The interaction is made easier through this integrated system as people are often difficult to structure through independent systems.

4.5.3 Certainty

Certainty involves the process that provides evidence that the design is justifiable and can be trusted. The proof can include past evidence or experiments in mimics and samples. CPS is structured to be capable of cooperating with new and unpredictable environments and functioning properly. It is expected to detect any unknown behavior by the system in order to study and improve the system.

4.5.4 Better System Performance

CPS provides enhanced system performance in terms of response and robotic redesign, thanks to the close interactivity between sensor devices and networked structure. Better assets and virtual subroutines ensure the availability of various sensing entities and various communication mechanisms, ultimately ensuring greater organizational execution by the CPS.

4.5.5 Expandability of System

CPS has the ability to expand structures according to demands, utilizing the resources of Cloud Computing appropriately. People can access essential infrastructure without requiring additional resources, which presents a great opportunity. CPS is inherently heterogeneous as it integrates physical kinetics with computational procedures. This ensures that demands are met in a timely manner, with efficiency and accuracy.

4.5.6 Autonomy

CPS is capable of providing autonomy as it includes sensor-cloud integration, which is essentially a closed-loop system. This allows sensor devices to perform calculations of physical kinetics, which are then utilized in the cyber subsystems. These subsystems ultimately drive the devices and apps, which in turn affect physical procedures.

4.5.7 Flexibility

Few existing systems based on the CPS provide greater flexibility compared to previous research efforts in the fields of WSN and cloud computing. CPS systems offer stability, improved functionality, and faster response times. This, in turn, enhances the overall flexibility of the CPS system.

4.5.8 Optimization

The biomedical sensors and cloud infrastructure that we currently have provide better optimizations for a variety of applications.

4.5.9 Better Response Time

The faster communication and processing abilities of the sensors and cloud infrastructure have led to better reaction times, offering early detection of any remote failures and the appropriate implementation of shared resources like bandwidth. Better response times also ensure security, and areas of improvement can be easily considered. When you know the areas in which you lag, you can improve the system for the greater good.

4.6 Conclusion and Future Scope

This chapter presents a detailed analysis of CPS in the healthcare sector. To ensure proper comprehension of MCPS, a recapitulation of CPS in a generalized manner is offered first. A taxonomy of CPS and MCPS is also provided, including eight different components. The classification above proves resourceful in realizing the trends, methods, and possible solutions related to certain apps in CPS. This will not only improve the faulty areas in the pre-existing system but also provide a horizon that reflects the future possibilities in CPS and MCPS.

The future scope of CPS in healthcare includes:

  1. Al Healthcare: Artificial intelligence (AI) is a booming trend that can be used efficiently when merged with the CPS system in healthcare. AI’s accuracy and intelligence can effectively manage emergency situations and even suggest solutions for them.
  2. Mobile Healthcare Services: Vehicles installed with advanced MCPS systems can have a significant impact on the development of the healthcare sector. People who suffer or are affected by the lack of services in remote areas can access this system through these mobile healthcare services.
  3. Smart Homes: It is a really good way of taking care of yourself or your loved ones. They will experience assisted living, and the instructions fed to the system will allow it to manage their health and provide timely reminders for medication consumption, routine checkups, and even call an ambulance in case of emergencies.

Part II Intelligent Decision-Making and Analysis Technology

Chapter 5 Significance of ML and NLP in Sentiment Analysis

PUMMY DHIMAN,4

Abstract

With the introduction of Web 2.0, sentiment analysis (SA) has expanded dramatically. The internet has provided a platform for people to express their opinions. Nowadays, individuals freely share their opinions on various topics online, and these reviews play a significant role in decisionmaking processes. The objective of SA is to utilize this data to gather crucial information about popular opinions, which can be used to enhance company choices, political campaigns, and consumer behavior. Consequently, SA has emerged as a new research area. In this study, a publicly available Twitter dataset was used and processed using NLTK to demonstrate the importance of the AI approach and machine learning (ML) in SA. Three classifiers were employed to binary classify the given input. Experimental results indicate that SVC achieves the highest accuracy.

5.1 Introduction

Sentiment refers to a person’s expression, emotions, attitude, or any opinion [1]. Nowadays, almost everyone exploits the internet or social media platforms to articulate their opinions on various matters, such as products, software, applications, movies, web series, and more. These opinions have become increasingly important as social networking sites, business models, and product managers utilize feedback forms to collect valuable customer feedback and analyze it for making better business decisions. Sentiment analysis (SA) is the process of understanding the text and identifying the user’s perspective. Through SA, text can be categorized into different sentiments, whether they are positive, negative, or neutral. This type of text classification is used by data analysts, business analysts, and others to quickly analyze collected data and feedback. There are three main methods for sentiment categorization: lexicon-based, machine learning (ML)/deep learning (DL), and hybrid [2], as shown in Figure 5.1. SA is a technique used to analyze a script or text in order to extract thoughts, opinions, and subjectivity, and determine the polarity [3], whether it is positive, negative, or neutral [1]. Opinion mining is another term for SA [4].

5.1.1 Lexicon-Based Approach

Three approaches are available for SA: the supervised method outperforms the lexicon-based method in terms of performance, and the hybrid approach combines the two. In the Lexicon approach, extracted expressions from the text are compared to its dictionary to provide a label. This method explains the (positive, negative, and neutral) polarity of the text content using a sentiment lexicon [5]. The attitudes of Twitter can be detected using various NLP techniques, including Stop Word Removing, Parts of Speech Tagging, name entity recognition (NER), and a large number of words, etc. Dictionaries are used as references in these methods. No training is required, hence less computing power is needed. With the aid of dictionaries, we are employing the lexicon approach to divide the text into two categories: “positive” and “negative” [24]. A dictionary-like collection of words, or a lexicon, is one where the words are grouped alphabetically. A dictionary-based method and a corpus-based approach are two variations of this strategy. Here, we are employing a corpus-based methodology. A corpus is a significant collection of words or text that analyzes the relationships between different languages and formulates a set of conceptual rules that govern a natural language from texts in that language [25].

5.1.2 ML/DL Approach

Deep learning (DL) and semantic ML both aim to create AI that mimics human capabilities such as indexing, verifying, predicting, and reasoning. Both disciplines have had a significant impact on the analysis of data and knowledge, as well as the related conceptual formulations. ML/DL uses a train-test split mechanism and pre-defined algorithms to classify sentiments.

5.1.3 Hybrid Approach

The hybrid method of SA employs both statistical and knowledge-based techniques for polarity detection. It benefits from the stability of the lexicon-based strategy and the superior accuracy of ML (statistical approaches). The process begins with a hybrid standard classification, which is then refined into a sophisticated technique that includes linguistic cataloging of semantic divergence modeled through fuzzy sets. The holistic approach to SA utilizes both statistical and knowledge-based methods for polarity identification. It benefits from the stability of the lexicon-based strategy and the superior accuracy of ML [6, 7].

5.2 Motivation

As we know, consumers and producers are mutually dependent. Consumer feedback is crucial in making better business decisions for producers [8]. However, analyzing people’s opinions is not an easy task. Sentiments also play a major role in influencing people’s minds. The availability of social platforms is being used to organize mass gatherings and oppose oppression. Social media can also be used to incite hatred against a class of people or a specific ethnic group, which can lead to a significant loss of life. Therefore, it is essential to have SA systems that can detect and mitigate such phenomena. Consequently, SA systems capable of detecting such phenomena are required. The goal of this study is to use ML techniques to predict the labels on a given dataset.

5.3 Previous Work

A critical task in natural language processing is emotion recognition from text, which has huge potential to advance several fields, including AI and human-computer interaction. In this chapter, the authors suggest labeling a large number of tweets according to their underlying sentiment. The researchers classify the emotions of an expression into positive or negative using DL algorithms. They conducted experiments on three separate data-sets, employing recurrent neural networks (RNNs) and long short-term memory to demonstrate high generalization ability and achieved an accuracy of 91.3% for both subclasses [9]. This chapter depicts the advancements in SA in Indian languages. The research compares SA carried out in various Indian languages, along with categorization methods based on lexicon, dictionary, and ML. Additionally, it lists the lexical resources that can be used for SA of Indian languages and discusses the challenges in creating such resources with limited availability [10]. This study contributes to the field of SA for the classification of consumer reviews, which is beneficial for analyzing a large amount of tweets with highly complex opinions that are either positive, negative, or neutral [11]. The goal of this effort is to develop a classifier using ML techniques that can predict the polarity of a comment. Their study consisted of three main steps: data extraction, processing, and modeling. They utilized the NLTK dataset to construct their model. The variables were then generated and processed using text mining algorithms. They conducted two experiments to assess the effectiveness of their model after creating a classifier to categorize tweets based on affirmative and pessimistic feelings [12]. This latest research focused on analyzing tweets related to the Uri massacre to evaluate sentiments and polarity. Manuscript removal algorithms were used to extract tweets for passion and polarization. A collection of frequently used words was created by preprocessing and recoding 5,000 tweets. R was implemented in this study to analyze polarity and emotions, with 94.3% of the research participants expressing loathing towards the Uri attack [13]. The goal of this chapter is to examine Indian citizens’ opinions on the government’s nationwide lockdown, which was implemented to curb the spread of the coronavirus. SA of tweets made by Indian residents was carried out using NLP and ML classifiers. A total of 12,741 tweets containing the phrase “India lockdown” were retrieved between April 5, 2020, and April 17, 2020. The Linear SVC classifier and unigrams were used in the experiment, achieving a precision of 84.4%. According to the study’s findings, the majority of the Indian population agrees with the government’s decision to impose a lockdown during the coronavirus outbreak [2]. Analyzing sentiment in raw text becomes particularly challenging when dealing with sarcastic text or the presence of both optimistic and pessimistic opinions in the same text. This model can be applied to various business models and provides an accuracy and precision of about 95% [1]. The study’s first finding is that an increase in tweet volume will inevitably lead to longer execution time for the algorithm. The second finding is that RF takes significantly longer to execute compared to SVM and NB. Therefore, this literature study systematically investigates and evaluates corporate and community white papers, academic research articles, journals, and reports to examine and analyze the concept of SA [14]. This study contributes to the field of SA for the classification of consumer reviews, which is beneficial for analyzing data from a large number of tweets with highly unstructured opinions that are either positive, negative, or neutral [12]. A technique called latent semantic analysis (LSA) examines text using precise mathematical computations and analyzes the connections between terms in the documents and between the documents in the corpus. LSA demonstrates that a single word can have multiple meanings, compress several concepts with the same semantics, and represent texts in a conceptual space with fewer dimensions [15]. Natural language processing (NLP) is the technique used in Twitter SA. In this project called Twitter SA, we explore the sentiments expressed in each tweet. We recognize three types of sentiments: positive, neutral, and negative. Initially, analyzing the emotions underlying each tweet posed the biggest challenge, but ML can now address this issue [21]. SA can be used to evaluate the positivity, negativity, or neutrality of written communication. People use various social media channels, such as Facebook and Twitter, where opinions can be accurately assessed.

This study utilizes a variety of ML techniques. Twitter is employed to analyze untrained data, which is then compared to trained software capable of determining neutrality, negativity, or positivity. Effective SA can be conducted on social networking data by removing subjective information from written material [22]. Classifying image sentiments based on raw photos and elusive human emotions can be a challenging task for computers. There is ample room for research in the field of image sentiment classification, especially in social data analytics. Nowadays, individuals often express their thoughts on the internet by uploading photographs to various social media platforms such as Facebook, Instagram, Twitter, and others. This research employs DL approaches to explore the latest advancements in image SA. In addition to DL algorithms, traditional ML approaches are also compared. The study suggests that a combination of rapid recurrent neural network and CNN can achieve high accuracy with minimal temporal complexity. According to previous studies [23], CNN achieves an average accuracy of approximately 96.50% in classifying emotions in a flicker picture collection.

5.4 Methodology

The following tools and methodology are utilized to accomplish the objective of SA. This research work used Python as the programming language, Anaconda as the programming environment, and various associated libraries such as Pandas, NumPy, NLTK, Scikit-learn, Matplotlib, etc. Figure 5.2 represents the research methodology flow. The research model will describe the different sections: (i) dataset collection; (ii) preprocessing; (iii) ML classifier; and (iv) performance metric evaluation.

5.4.1 Dataset

The dataset utilized in this study consists of 31,962 labeled tweets in CSV format. It is available in the public domain and includes entries for tweet ID, label, and the tweet itself. This dataset contains short-length tweets, with a label of ‘1’ indicating a racist (positive) tweet and ‘0’ indicating a non-racist (negative) tweet.

5.4.2 Software-Hardware Requirement

Intel(R) Core(TM) i3-5005U CPU @ 2.00 GHz 2.00 GHz, 8 GB RAM, Windows OS, Anaconda IDE, and Python version 3.10 were used to carry out this study. The experiment utilized Pandas, NumPy, Matplotlib, NLTK, and re libraries.

5.4.3 Pre-Processing

Preprocessing raw sentiment texts was necessary before feeding them into the models. The first step was to eliminate stop words. Afterwards, words are spelled correctly to clean up the dataset. Throughout the text, white space is used to separate each paragraph, and words are stemmed to remove suffixes. Finally, the word tokens are separated with white space to present a clean corpus, which had been tokenized later for use in the models. As shown in Figure 5.3, it is preprocessed using the NLTK Python library to deal with NLP [16] tasks. Certain patterns are removed in the next step using the sub() method, and then the removal of Twitter handles is managed.

After this, if there are any special characters present in the given strings, they have been removed using NLTK methods [17]. Removal of shorter words, less than 3 characters, has taken place. Tokenization, i.e., dividing the given string into tokens, is taken care of in the next step. Later on, the stem() method is used for stemming purposes. To find out various patterns, exploratory data analysis (EDA) is also done. It is necessary to vectorize the data after the data cleaning step has been completed. The text is transformed into a numerical two-dimensional matrix through vectorization in order to reduce time complexity [18].

After the pre-processing step, the dataset needs to be split into two parts, namely the train-test split. ML algorithms are trained on datasets (called training data) and optimized to find specific patterns or outputs in the data. Rules and data structures are the components of a learned ML model. The first part is for training purposes, i.e., to build the model, and the next split is for testing the built model. In this case, 75% of the data is used for training and 25% for testing. The experiment uses tweets and labels. After pre-processing, the data is fed into supervised ML classifiers [19, 20]. A ML algorithm is a mathematical method for detecting patterns in data sets. Statistics, calculus, and linear algebra are frequently used in ML algorithms. Linear regression, decision trees (DT), random forest (RF), SVM, and XGBoost are some popular ML algorithms. For this purpose, three supervised ML classifiers are used: logistic regression (LR), SVM, and decision tree:

  1. Logistic Regression (LR): It is used to determine whether or not an input belongs to a specific group.
  2. Support Vector Machines (SVMs): These group objects according to their common features by generating coordinates in an n-dimensional space.
  3. Decision Trees (DT): These like classifiers, is used to determine which category an input belongs to by traversing the tree’s leaves and nodes.

5.5 Performance Metrics (PM)

The efficiency of the model was evaluated using the following performance parameters: accuracy, precision, recall, and F1-score. Based on Table 5.1, all these parameters are derived from the confusion matrix shown in Figure 5.4: true positives (TP), false positives (FP), true negatives (TN), and false negatives (FNs).

Figure 5.4 Confusion matrix.
Predicted Predicted

Actual

 TP FN

Actual

 FP TN
Table 5.1 Parameter Explanation
Parameter Explanation
TP Positive prediction and actually it is also positive.
FP Positive prediction but in actual it is negative.
TN Negative prediction and actually it is also negative.
FN Negative prediction but in actual it is positive.

Table 5.1 gives the explanation of confusion matrix parameters.

  1. Accuracy (TN + TP/TP + FP + FN + TN) = Identifies the positive predictions to the total number of predictions.
  2. Precision (TP/TP + FP) = Determines how often a modal predicts the positive class correctly.
  3. Recall (TP/TP + FN) = Identifies out of all positive labels, how many did the modal correctly identify.
  4. F1-Score (2*(Precision*Recall)/(Precision + Recall)) = Harmonic mean of precision and recall.

5.6 Result

This section addresses the following research question – how accurate are traditional ML models in analyzing sentiments in the given dataset? After pre-processing the dataset, ML algorithms were applied, and a supervised learning approach yielded satisfactory results for the classification of Twitter reviews. Table 5.2 reports the performances of various traditional ML models. For LR, the achieved TP is 7362, FP is 348, FN is 65, and TN is 215. For Decision Tree Classifier, TP is 7131, FP is 255, FN is 296, and TN is 308. For SVC, TP is 7394, FP is 372, FN is 33, and TN is 191.

5.7 Conclusion

In today’s digital era, we have information at our fingertips. We engage in online shopping, watch web series, and share our opinions online to inform others about products. We also rely on reviews before making purchases or decisions. As a result, online reviews have sparked extensive research on sentiment analysis (SA) in recent years. These comments are written in human language, which computers struggle to understand. This is where NLP plays a significant role in bridging this gap. The Twitter SA dataset has been explored using ML models to classify tweets as positive or negative sentiment and compared in terms of accuracy. However, there is still room for improvement in achieving more efficient results by utilizing transformer-based techniques and hybrid approaches.

Chapter 6 Intelligent Innovative Systems: Experience in Multidisciplinary Outlook on Current Challenges and Contingency

N. INDUMATHI,5

Advanced technologies have practically reformed every aspect of human existence, including the implementation of machine learning (ML) innovations such as intelligent decision-making systems across various domains. In the present era, ML applications are being sought after for deployment in fields like medicine, industrial safety, and agriculture, with the aim of predicting future events. In the medical field, computer-aided decision support systems and machine intelligence can be easily utilized for disease prediction and diagnosis. These systems learn important features from biomedical datasets and make predictions based on new data. Similarly, the effectiveness of ML in predicting future outcomes has been recognized in industrial safety management. Proactive safety management requires the use of leading indicators to monitor and evaluate the effectiveness of protective measures, aiming to prevent incidents before they occur. In the agricultural sector, ML intelligent systems can assist farmers in optimizing crop yield by considering factors such as climate conditions, rainfall, soil conditions, irrigation systems, and pest and disease management. To address these challenges, this chapter proposes an intelligent approach for predicting accidents in industries, diagnosing diseases in medical settings, and predicting crop yield in agriculture. This chapter employs different ML algorithms and powerful classifiers to recognize potentially hazardous project characteristics and site conditions, predict diabetic retinal diseases, breast cancer, histopathology, tissue segmentation, cardiac diseases, chronic kidney diseases, and brain tumors at an early stage, and develop crop productivity as well as its quality in the agriculture sector. The system is further validated through statistical measures to determine the efficiency of the algorithms by comparing predictive accuracy and error rates.

6.1 Introduction

In recent days, the computer-aided decision support system has implemented machine learning (ML) approaches and has been practically applied in multidisciplinary fields. This system learns the important features of biomedical datasets and makes predictions based on new data. ML implementation now includes two-dimensional and three-dimensional images. ML-based algorithms are capable of predicting accidents and future safety measures to minimize accidents. However, these methods were developed for specific leading indicators, limiting their application as the impact on safety performance varies between organizations and projects. Research on predicting fire accidents aims to encourage the implementation of targeted security controls and improve safety performance. The main goal of precision agriculture is to improve crop yield. Crop growth and yield depend on environmental attributes such as climate variables, soil parameters, irrigation, pest, and disease management. ML algorithms and powerful classifiers have been developed to predict biomedical imaging and signaling, predict accidents, and predict crop productivity in the fields of medical, safety performance, and agriculture.

Diabetic retinopathy is one of the leading irreparable retinal diseases affecting vision [1]. Early-stage detection is crucial to prevent vision loss. The diagnosis of DR is achieved through the identification of exudates in fundus images. Classification prediction of DR is determined using ensemble ML algorithms such as AdaBoost, naïve bayes (NB), random forest (RF), and support vector machine (SVM) [2]. Deep learning (DL) convolutional neural networks (CNN), specifically GoogLeNet and AlexNet ImageNet models, are used for feature extraction with solvers like SGD, Adam, AdaGrad, NAG, and RMSprop to achieve a maximum accuracy of 74.5% [3]. Optical coherence tomography (OCT) images are utilized for effective classification of diabetic retinal diseases including Diabetic macular edema, DR, and age-related macular edema (AMD). The customized CNN OCT-NET, based on DL, achieves a precision of 93% and an Area Under Curve of 0.99 on the SD-OCT public dataset SERI-CUHK [4]. Diabetic macular edema affects the subretinal fluid (SRF) and the pigment epithelium detachment (PME) of the retina. A deep neural network combined with atrous spatial pyramid pooling is used for OCT image segmentation in DME [5], while a convolutional network is employed for exudates-based DME detection [6]. ML decision tree classifiers are applied for detecting diabetic macular edema lumps, achieving performance measures of 92% accuracy, 89.8% sensitivity, and 94.8% specificity [7].

The use of ML calculations requires a tremendous amount of data to trigger dynamic processes in various mechanical cycles. In this way, the implementation of innovative principles such as Cyber-physical systems and IoT enables the generation of different types of indigenous knowledge, as observed in the field of big data analytics. Generally, data follows a specific life cycle (source, collection, storage, processing, visualization, transmission, application). However, most of the time, the collected data that will be processed in subsequent steps is mixed with noisy data generated from the overall environment, making it difficult to separate the original dataset from the noise [8].

According to the statistical report by Liakos et al. [9], ML models are applied in various applications in agriculture such as crop yield prediction (20%), soil management (61%), and pest and disease detection (22%). In most cases, machine-based predictions obtained accurate forecasting in many aspects. It also proved that most agricultural studies implemented the artificial neural network model (ANN) and SVM model to make better decisions. Most probably, ANN models are used for implementation in crop improvement, soil, and water administration. When ML is implemented with sensor data, the models are evolving into artificial intelligence (AI) systems to provide valuable insights for making informed decisions with the definitive goal of enhancing crop productivity.

ML techniques can be utilized in the field of soil management to extract soil parameters. They can also be applied to predict crop growth based on factors such as soil moisture, weather conditions, and irrigation features. As a result, ML methods have contributed to efficient automatic irrigation [10]. Many farmers still follow traditional crop development systems, which may lead to decreased production in cases of insufficient rainfall. To enhance crop productivity, ML techniques with hybrid algorithms have been implemented, yielding promising results [11]. This chapter provides an overview of ML models used in various fields including medicine, safety management, and agriculture. The discussion focuses on how these models efficiently classify medical image data, analyze and predict fire accidents at an early stage, and identify and monitor crop canopy characteristics, soil humidity, pests, and diseases.

6.2 Methods and Discussions

6.2.1 Machine Learning (ML) in Medical Held

6.2.1.1 Diabetic Retinal Diseases Detection

Diabetic retinopathy is one of the leading irreparable retinal diseases affecting vision [12]. Detecting the early stage is crucial in preventing vision loss. The diagnosis of DR is achieved through the detection of exudates in the fundus image, as depicted in Figure 6.1. The classification and prediction of DR are determined using ensemble ML algorithms such as AdaBoost, NB, RF, and SVM [13].

OCT images are used for the effective classification of diabetic retinal diseases such as Diabetic macular edema, DR, and AMD (Figure 6.2). The DL customized CNN OCT-NET is used to achieve a precision of 93% and an Area Under Curve of 0.99 on the SD-OCT public dataset SERI-CUBK [14].

Diabetic macular edema affects the sub-retinal fluid (SRF) and PME of the retina. A deep neural network combined with atrous spatial pyramid pooling is used to segment the OCT image for DME [15], while a convolutional network is used for exudates-based DME detection. A ML decision tree classifier is applied to detect the diabetic macular edema lump, with performance measures of 92% accuracy, 89.8% sensitivity, and 94.8% specificity [16].

6.2.1.2 Diagnosis of Breast Cancer

Breast cancer has been one of the common diseases affecting women worldwide. In 2018, approximately 15% of women died due to breast cancer, according to the World Health Organization (WHO) [17]. Early detection is crucial in order to prevent fatalities. ML algorithms have been utilized to detect breast cancer in mammogram images (Figure 6.3). A CNN, along with optimization methods, has been employed to classify the histopathological image dataset [18]. The performance of the classifier was evaluated based on the identification of malignant tumors, using techniques such as normalization, weight regularization, shallow SoftMax regression, and shallow logistic regression (LR) [19].

Multiple instance learning is used to identify histopathological images of breast cancer. A two-step DL model is employed for mammogram image classification. A deep convolutional pre-trained network is applied to achieve multi-view classification of mammogram images with an ROC of 0.9 [20].

Transfer learning techniques were implemented to achieve an AUC of 0.81 in the classification of breast cancer tumor mammographic images [21]. An extreme learning machine (ELM) technique was applied to diagnose the breast cancer mammographic dataset with an accuracy of 96.02% [20]. Tele mammographic images have played an important role in telehealth monitoring systems. ML was implemented using a J48 decision tree, RF, k-nearest neighbor (KNN), and multilayer perceptron (MLPs) [21].

6.2.1.3 Brain Tumor Detection

Brain tumors are defined as the mass growth of abnormal cells in the brain, which can lead to death. Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) is used for detecting this type of tumor (Figure 6.4). Nowadays, computer-aided systems are used for easy and fast diagnosis. CNN kernel-based algorithms are implemented to segment the MRI brain images [22]. Deep neural network classifiers are applied to classify the types of brain tumors. The popular SVM ML algorithm is used for classifying brain MRIs. Auto multi-task medical image segmentation is demonstrated in the form of a single CNN that includes three tasks: brain MRL Breast MRL and cardiac CTA [23].

6.2.2 Machine Learning (Ml) in the Industrial Safety Performance Sector

Humanity has achieved enormous advances in industrial manufacturing during the last 300 years. The third industrial revolution, which began in the 1950s, increased digitalization through the use of semiconductors and, more recently, communication networks, which made automated development possible. AI and ML have been extensively used in the manufacturing sector during the past 10 years, enabling more efficient operations, less resource waste and sustainable resource utilization, better working conditions, and improved quality and productivity. By allowing defect identification and prediction, optimum use of raw materials and resources, leveraging heterogeneous big data analysis, and networked manufacturing facilities, AI/ML-based manufacturing may bring about a number of industrial advancements [24].

6.2.2.1 Occupational Risk Prediction

A branch of machine language is based on “deep learning” and demonstrates how some of the gaps in risk assessment can be addressed. ML refers to computer programming techniques for learning through experience. Through DL techniques, a computer can be trained to evaluate risk for safety-critical industries such as fireworks manufacturing, mining industries, and oil and gas. This involves analyzing a large volume of data, including indicators from regular operations and past incidents, ranging from mishaps to major accidents. Due to the subjective nature of the risk concept, professional oversight is required as the degree of risk cannot be applied with certainty to each case. This supervised learning enables DL. Once the model has been trained for risk categorization, it utilizes its expertise to assess major risks based on the condition of the controlled device, such as a fireworks, mining, or oil and gas field [25]. Figure 6.5 represents the development of DL for industrial risk assessment.

6.2.2.1.1 Classification and Regression (CART)

The classification and regression tree (CART) is a selected tree-based information extraction methodology used by numerous scientists for studying work-related accidents. The CART framework aims to establish a relationship between accident severity, working environmental factors, risk factors, and accident factors. By utilizing CART, it is possible to determine the anticipated scenarios and logical outcomes of business interactions within a real-world industry sector incidents [26].

6.2.2.1.2 Support Vector Machine (SVM)

Nonlinear kernel SVMs have been successfully and often used in supervised classification due to their capability to represent data in the required space where groups are clearly distinguishable. SVM algorithms have shown comparable or higher accuracy in regression and classification tasks compared to other empirical ML methods. SVM statistical models have very reliable output on binary classification issues [27].

The explanation for this is the SVM algorithm, which determines an optimum boundary capable of separating the two groups of the target variable. The three popular meta-heuristic algorithms for SVM include linear, radial basis function, and the degree d of the probability density function [28].

6.2.2.1.3 Decision Tree (DT)

Decision trees (DTs) have become more common because they are clear, easy to interpret, and efficient classification algorithms The key reason for using DTs is to analyze quantity and quality trends in the data for the purpose of exploring hidden knowledge. Among the different classifications of DT algorithms, boosting has been considered one of the most significant developments in ML over the last 20 years, as it can transform a group of different learners [29].

6.2.2.1.4 Naïve Bayes (NB)

Bayesian classifiers are based on Bayes’ theorem, which uses conditional probabilities to determine the likelihood of the dependent variable given the independent variables. The Naive Bayes classifier is one of the most widely used classifiers in AI. It simplifies the complexity of Bayesian classifiers by making the naive assumption of independence, reducing the number of parameters to be evaluated from 2^(2n-1) to just 2n when modeling P(X), where X is the independent variable, Y is the dependent variable, and n is the number of independent variables used in the experiment [30].

6.2.2.1.5 Reinforcement Learning (RL)

Specific reference to the unsupervised learning method involves determining if the chosen action resulted in a reward for a specific performance metric. Reinforcement learning (RL) looks at sequential actions and seeks out their outcomes, selecting those that are best suited to the problem at hand. Thus, RL is very different from other types of learning, which concentrate on the utilization of historical information, the production of knowledge from previous decisions and rewards [31]. The ML categories and key aspects are demonstrated in Figure 6.6.

6.2.2.2 Predictive Model For Safety Domain

Security leading indicators are an important component of proactive safety management and provide useful information on the performance of organizational protection [32]. Safety professionals have concluded that careful collection, evaluation, and mitigation of leading indicators would lead to real functional improvements [33]. Criteria have been established for selecting useful leading indicators, including validity, reliability, responsiveness, representativeness, openness to bias, and cost-effectiveness [34]. Figure 6.7 represents the key parameters identified in industrial safety and accidents.

Additionally, effective safety measures should be numerically assessed, updated frequently, and reflect the chosen overall safety determinants [35]. The potential impact of interaction related to the aggregation and analysis of multiple leading indicators is believed to yield the greatest anticipated outcome [34]. Previous studies suggest that promoting a zero-injury state and predictive protection models require more than just personal protective equipment (PPE) and proactive preventive strategies [37]. Performance may play a crucial role in bridging the gap. Companies will be able to develop accident prevention plans more swiftly and efficiently if they can anticipate and prepare for potential improvements in safety performance [38].

The capacity of a single company to lower accident rates via the use of cutting-edge and predictive analytics in conjunction with a rising safety data collection. To proactively anticipate where and when occupational injuries may occur, a variety of predictive models have been created [39]. Various models should be established to forecast construction safety performance globally. They have demonstrated that the diversity of claims and privilege instances in all built models, as well as the number of firms in every category of eligible profits, all predict the safety outcome. The prevalence of firms in various categories of responsible earnings and the frequency of workplace injuries were also shown to be positively and pertinently correlated [40].

To evaluate the precision of risk variables in predicting safety performance, employ a linear prediction model utilizing principal component analysis (PCA). To thoroughly analyze safety risk factors in infrastructure projects and ascertain the probability of a project’s safety status, World Steel Association [41] proposed a hybrid system based on Bayesian networks. While their approach proved effective in forecasting safety outcomes, it was limited to small-scale projects.

6.2.2.3 Prediction of Occupational Accidents

Occupational injuries are a significant concern across all sectors. According to statistics provided by the International Labor Organization (ILO), there are over 317 million reported incidents and hazardous events at work annually, resulting in more than 2 3 million premature deaths each year. In India alone, it is reported that over 48,000 employees die annually due to work-related accidents, and nearly 37 million cases result in at least 3 days of sickness absence. The study also reveals a fatal accident rate of 114 per 1,000,000 employees and an accident rate of 8,700 per 1,000,000 employees in India [38].

As per the World Steel Association, the protection and health of workers are important for sustainability [42]. Occupational risk is defined by two parameters: one that shows the incidence of injuries and the other that relates to the severity of injuries. Occupational accident reports can be classified in a format that includes parameters such as the year of the accident, place, workers’ criteria, age of the workers, time of the incidents, description of the accidents, etc. The above criteria include the type of injury, the substance factor involved, the types of injury, the part of the body impacted, the hazardous condition involved in the incident, and the hazardous activity involved in the incident.

The optimization approach is to create a measurement model that uses the GA methodology to determine occupational hazards, taking into account the magnitude and frequency of occupational injuries. It is also reported that safety in the industry is correlated with higher performance, and the most effective steelmakers are also found to be the safest. Therefore, it is a major concern today that, while we have sufficient experience of severe, harmful, and time-loss incidents, we may not learn from past records. If we could predict future instances of occurrences using historical evidence, this would help the concerned industry. Forecasts have also been produced in the field of workplace injury analysis.

It has been described as an essential strategy that is focused on the fundamental premise that incidents can be avoided if they can be expected. As a consequence, the prediction of workplace injuries in the practice of safety analysis at work has been shown to be important in order to minimize the risk of injuries that could, in turn, occur [43].

6.2.2.4 Occupational Models

Identify the new technologies that could be associated to ML [44]:

  1. Predictive Maintenance: The ability to accurately predict challenges to the manufacturing line prior to an interruption is invaluable to the manufacturer. It enables the employer to plan downtime during the most suitable times and reduce unnecessary delays. Unscheduled interruptions heavily impact gross profit and can result in the loss of the consumer base. The implementation of ML for predictive maintenance is expected to increase by around 38% across factories, as it has the potential to improve gross profit by eliminating unscheduled shutdowns.
  2. Personal Protective Equipment (PPE): It is a device that protects individuals from health and safety hazards. Failing to wear PPE significantly increases the risk of injury and, in some cases, can result in financial penalties for injuries or even the loss of life for staff members who do not wear protective gear, such as face masks and safety glasses. The implementation of ML for PPE identification allows companies to minimize the risk of worker injuries, prevent accidents in the workplace, improve operational efficiency, and gather data on workplace safety.
  3. Smart Manufacturing: A key objective of AI and AI is to enable the development of a digital replica of the manufacturing floor. The creation of the digital twin would occur through an effort under a model-based systems engineering process (MBSE) using AI algorithms and acquired data as a foundation. The digital twin would serve as a platform for conducting “what if” scenarios to uncover unknowns. Additionally, the digital twin can be used as an end-to-end model for designing higher reliability components and optimizing the interactions between production line machines to enhance performance [45].
  4. Machine Human Interaction: The division of labor between humans and machines is typically observed in technologically advanced operational settings, and the jobs assigned are classified as machine- and human-specific. Unlike the concept of computer-integrated manufacturing (CIM), Industry 4.0 aims to combine people and machines in a collaborative workspace within a human-cyber-physical framework (HCPS), as opposed to a worker-less production facility [26].
  5. Open Access: The most recent advancements in ML-driven techniques and the emergence of DL have opened up new opportunities to revolutionize Industry 4.0 and beyond. These advancements have led to the possibility of achieving highly autonomous and efficient industrial operations, while improving fault detection, mitigating human errors, ensuring security, and preventing unexpected losses, even in complex and dynamic environments [43].

The integration of AI and ML in various aspects of human society has played a crucial role in enabling exciting new opportunities to enhance quality of life and preserve the sustainability of our ecosystem. In the context of Industry 4.0, AI/ML enables numerous improvements across the manufacturing sector, enhancing processes such as waste reduction, automation, and product quality. A highly significant area in realizing the vision of Industry 4.0 is the timely detection and prevention of faults, utilizing solutions from the industrial internet of things, and the subsequent acquisition and processing of diverse large-scale data [46]. Figure 6.8 illustrates the use of ML algorithms for industrial risk assessment prediction and prevention.

6.3 Machine Learning (ML) in Agri-Technology Domain

6.3.1 Weather and Crop Prediction

The yield of crops is primarily based on weather conditions and soil variables such as pH value and nutrients. Weather parameters like temperature, solar radiation, relative humidity, and rainfall play a significant role in the growth and development of crops. Forecasting crop yield is challenging due to the fluctuation of weather variables in meteorological data. Recurrent neural networks (RNNs) can be effectively used in weather forecasting [47]. Additionally, crops can be classified based on weather using classification algorithms such as SVM, C4.5, C5.0, CART, and RF, as shown in Figure 6.9. Although RNN successfully processes time series data, integrating RNN with a statistical model like generalized autoregressive conditional heteroskedasticity (GARCH) enhances the prediction process. Compared to conventional neural networks, RNN allows the output from the previous step to be forwarded to the current step, enabling the hidden layers in RNN to remember the sequence of the network. Therefore, the values are associated with each other [48]. Before classifying crops or forecasting weather, data preprocessing is necessary to fill in missing values in the climatic or crop data.

6.3.2 Pest and Disease Management

Crops must be protected from pests and diseases to enhance crop yield through efficient ML methods. ML decision tree algorithms like C4.0 and C5.0 can be used to classify agricultural crop pest images. Both algorithms construct a decision tree using a training set of data [49]. Accuracy prediction is done using a split testing set of data. C4.5 and C5.0 are both effective classifiers for constructing DT and generating sets of rules. The C4.5 algorithm uses a depth-first strategy during construction, allowing for pruning and compact handling of missing and noisy data. However, C5.0 outperforms C4.5 in terms of processing speed, memory, and efficiency in some cases [50]. CNNs are a type of neural network that can be applied to classify pest images in the agricultural field. Feature extraction in pest images is achieved through adaptive learning of neurons in the neural network [51]. The flow chart for pest recognition and classification is schematically represented in Figure 6.10.

6.3.3 Soil and Irrigation Management

All crops grow better depending on the type of soil, nutrients, and irrigation. Therefore, it is important to choose the appropriate soil for planting in order to maximize crop yield. Changes in soil and weather conditions have a definite impact on the water management system, leading to a decrease in crop yield. Therefore, it is recommended to use intelligent automatic irrigation systems that utilize soil sensor data and weather data, as shown in Figure 6.11. The soil moisture level data is transmitted to the cloud via a Wi-Fi modem. ML classification techniques are employed to classify irrigation strategies based on the soil data. As a result, water usage by automatic irrigation systems can be reduced while achieving a high accuracy model [36].

6.4 Conclusion

Machine learning (ML) models have advanced rapidly over the past few years. The development of these algorithms is likely to provide more meaningful datasets, which, when integrated with complex algorithmic results, pave the way for better prediction and decision-making in the fields of medicine, safety management, and agriculture. Numerous ML models have already been intelligently implemented in various domains. This chapter predominantly reveals that:

  • CNN and other DL models with optimization offer an effective image classification in detecting cancer and tumors.
  • Classification, regression, and network models are appropriate for predicting fire accidents in the cracker’s city at the earliest stage. These models are considered promising tools for conducting regression analysis of risk assessment in industries, enabling the measurement of accidents, and providing valuable knowledge for safety management measures.
  • Machine learning models such as SVM, Decision tree, NB, and neural networks enhance the prediction of crop yield, soil management, irrigation systems, pests, and disease management.
